Derecho.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThese instructions combine information from both the \u003ca href=\"https://www2.mmm.ucar.edu/projects/mpas/tutorial/Virtual2026/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eMPAS Tutorial - Practice Guide\u003c/a\u003e and the specific instructions for running with GOCART-2G aerosols. Be sure to go to the \u003ca href=\"https://mpas-dev.github.io/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eMPAS Home page\u003c/a\u003e and \u003ca href=\"https://www2.mmm.ucar.edu/projects/mpas/site/index.html\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eMPAS-Atmosphere webpage\u003c/a\u003e where there is a lot more information and documentation.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eTable of Contents\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-table-of-contents\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Table of Contents\" href=\"#table-of-contents\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eBackground chapter\u003c/strong\u003e provides a brief description of the MPAS-GOCART-2G model.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eChapter 1\u003c/strong\u003e gives instructions on running a case where all the input files are provided. It allows the new user to become familiar with the workflow in preparing a MPAS-GOCART2G simulation and is great for ensuring that the user has configured their system properly.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eChapter 2\u003c/strong\u003e provides information on setting up your own case using the same grid mesh as in chapter 1 but running a different time period, including where to obtain input files and running the program \u003cem\u003einit_atmosphere\u003c/em\u003e which sets up the MPAS-GOCART2G simulation.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eChapter 3\u003c/strong\u003e provides information on running with a variable resolution grid mesh and setting up different variable resolution grids.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eChapter 4\u003c/strong\u003e provides instructions for viewing MPAS-GOCART2G output using uxarray in Python scripts.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eAppendix A\u003c/strong\u003e explains how to download MERRA2 files.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eAppendix B\u003c/strong\u003e describes ways to visualize MPAS-GOCART2G output.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eQuick workflow guide for first-time users\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-quick-workflow-guide-for-first-time-users\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Quick workflow guide for first-time users\" href=\"#quick-workflow-guide-for-first-time-users\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eIf you are new to MPAS-GOCART2G, use this order:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003col dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eRead the background section only to understand the model and terminology.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eComplete Chapter 1 to build and test the example case with pre-provided inputs.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eUse Chapter 2 to prepare the files needed for your own case.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eOnce the case is set up, verify that the generated \u003ccode\u003enamelist.*\u003c/code\u003e and \u003ccode\u003estreams.*\u003c/code\u003e files are in the run directory.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eSubmit the job and check model output files before trying a larger simulation.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ol\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eBackground chapter Familiarizing yourself with the physics suite to run a MPAS-GOCART2G simulation\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-background-chapter-familiarizing-yourself-with-the-physics-suite-to-run-a-mpas-gocart2g-simulation\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Background chapter Familiarizing yourself with the physics suite to run a MPAS-GOCART2G simulation\" href=\"#background-chapter-familiarizing-yourself-with-the-physics-suite-to-run-a-mpas-gocart2g-simulation\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe MPAS-GOCART2G model has been developed as part of the NSF NCAR’s vision to move towards a unified modeling framework and links the MPAS-A dynamical core (Skamarock et al., 2012) to the GOCART2G chemical mechanism (Collow et al., 2024). The GOCART2G module adds 30 new scalars to represent eight tropospheric aerosol species and their precursors. Major aerosol species represented by GOCART2G include black carbon (BC), brown carbon (BrC), organic carbon (OC), sulfate, nitrate, ammonium, dust, and sea-salt. BC, BrC, and OC are present in hydrophobic and hydrophilic modes with an e-folding lifetime of 2.5 days for conversion of hydrophobic mode to hydrophilic mode. All the aerosol species are subjected to gravitational settling, dry deposition, and wet deposition processes. Gravitational settling is calculated following the parameterization of Fuchs (1965). Dry deposition velocity of aerosol and precursor species is estimated using the Wesely (1989) scheme. Resolved scale wet deposition of aerosols is calculated using aerosol-aware Thompson-Eidhammer 2014 scheme. Convective transport of all chemical constituents and wet deposition is simulated using nTiedtke scheme.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe implementation of GOCART-2G is illustrated in Figure 1 and can be categorized into four layers as the Input Layer, the Model Initialization and Temporal Update Layer, The Physics and Dynamics Layer, and the Chemistry and Diagnostics layer:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\":alt: The MPAS-A, chemistry, and external tools system flowchart\n:align: center\n:width: 100%\n\n**Figure 1.** The MPAS-A system (gray and blue boxes) and chemistry and GOCART-2G system (green and yellow boxes).\"\u003e\u003cpre lang=\"{figure}\" class=\"notranslate\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003e:alt: The MPAS-A, chemistry, and external tools system flowchart\n:align: center\n:width: 100%\n\n**Figure 1.** The MPAS-A system (gray and blue boxes) and chemistry and GOCART-2G system (green and yellow boxes).\n\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eAnthropogenic emissions are represented using the CAMS emissions inventory (Soulie et al., 2023). BC emissions from all sources are assumed to be 80% in hydrophobic mode while BrC and OC emissions are assumed to be 50% in hydrophobic mode at the time of emissions. The remaining fractions are considered to be hydrophilic. Anthropogenic emissions from the aviation sector (if provided as input to the model) are distributed in three layers: Landing and Take off emissions are assigned to the 100 m layer, Continuous Climb and Descent Operations (CDO) emissions are assigned to 100 m - 9 km layer and the remaining aircraft flight operations are distributed between 9 and 10 km altitude layers. Emissions from all other anthropogenic sources are emitted within the lowest 100 m.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eBiogenic emissions are read from pre-calculated emissions files available in the CAMS inventory (i.e., they are \u003cem\u003enot\u003c/em\u003e computed at each emission time step with inputs from local temperature and PAR). CAMS v4 biogenic emissions include only isoprene emissions, therefore CAMS v3.1 is used and CAMS 2019 biogenic emissions are applied to MPAS-GOCART2G simulations for case studies for all years. Following Collow et al. (2024), isoprene, monoterpene, and other terpene emissions are assigned to the hydrophilic mode of the OC. Since their emissions occur at the tree top, they are only emitted in the lowest model layer.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eBiomass burning emissions in MPAS-GOCART-2G are represented using FINN version 2.5.1 (Wiedinmyer et al., 2023). A climatological diurnal profile is applied to distribute the emissions to hourly values. MPAS-GOCART-2G then uniformly mixes the biomass burning emissions within the PBL. To ensure model stability and prevent unrealistically high Aerosol Optical Depth (AOD) values from biomass burning, the model limits biomass burning emissions in such a way that AOD from biomass burning emissions over all time steps in a day cannot exceed AOD = 30.0.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eDust aerosols are represented using five size bins (radii of 0.73, 1.4, 2.4, 4.5, and 8.0 µm) and their emissions are calculated online within the model utilizing the Ginoux et al. (2001) parameterization but with the dimensionless factor C set to 0.65 µg s\u003csup\u003e2\u003c/sup\u003e m\u003csup\u003e-5\u003c/sup\u003e as in (Zhao et al., 2010). Static geographical fields such as “erod” (erodibility representing the areas from where dust aerosols can be emitted), clayfrac and sandfrac required for dust emissions are mapped to the MPAS-GOCART domain along with the processing of other static geographical fields (see Section 1.3 of \u003ca href=\"https://www2.mmm.ucar.edu/projects/mpas/tutorial/Virtual2026/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eMPAS Tutorial - Practice Guide\u003c/a\u003e for processing these fields). Sea-salt aerosols also use five size bins, specifically with radii of 0.079, 0.316, 1.119, 2.818, and 7.772 µm. Emissions for sea-salt are calculated using the Gong (2003) wind-driven parameterization, but this includes two key modifications: first, friction velocity has replaced the 10m wind speed, which is required for tuning the parameterization's constants; and second, a correction term dependent on sea surface temperature was added. This temperature-dependent modification is similar to the approach by Jaegle et al. (2011) but was specifically tuned to improve agreement between the simulated sea-salt AOD and MODIS-retrieved AOD. The GOCART2G’s default scale-aware factor of 0.6 at grid spacings exceeding 200 km to 0.35 at grid spacings less than 12 km has been applied to model sea-salt emissions as a function of model grid spacing.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eReferences\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-references\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: References\" href=\"#references\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eCollow, A. Phys., 10, 8821–8838, \u003ca href=\"https://doi.org/10.5194/acp-10-8821-2010\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003ehttps://doi.org/10.5194/acp-10-8821-2010\u003c/a\u003e, 2010.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003ePhysics Configuration with MPAS-GOCART2G\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-physics-configuration-with-mpas-gocart2g\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Physics Configuration with MPAS-GOCART2G\" href=\"#physics-configuration-with-mpas-gocart2g\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eA new physics suite named \"MPAS-GOCART2G\" has been developed based on the “convection_permitting” MPAS-A physics suite. Please see the physics component of the namelist.atmosphere below. The PBL mixing of chemical constituents is calculated inside the MYNN PBL scheme. Note that deposition velocity of chemical constituents is still calculated by GOCART-2G and is passed to MYNN for deposition along with the turbulent mixing. Convective transport of tracers is performed using the nTiedtke scheme. In addition to setting these options in the physics part of the namelist, additional variables need to be set in the “chemistry” part of the namelist (see in bold font below under “chemistry” components). GOCART-2G aerosols also interact with the Thompson cloud microphysics and RRTMG radiation schemes.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u0026amp;physics\u003cbr\u003e\nconfig_sst_update = false\u003cbr\u003e\nconfig_sstdiurn_update = false\u003cbr\u003e\nconfig_deepsoiltemp_update = false\u003cbr\u003e\nconfig_radtlw_interval = '00:30:00'\u003cbr\u003e\nconfig_radtsw_interval = '00:30:00'\u003cbr\u003e\nconfig_bucket_update = 'none'\u003cbr\u003e\nconfig_physics_suite = 'MPAS-GOCART2G'\u003cbr\u003e\nconfig_microp_scheme = 'mp_thompson_gocart2G'\u003cbr\u003e\nconfig_pbl_scheme = 'bl_mynn'\u003cbr\u003e\nconfig_mynn_mixchems = true\u003cbr\u003e\nconfig_convection_scheme = 'cu_ntiedtke'\u003cbr\u003e\nconfig_ntiedtke_ctrchems = true\u003cbr\u003e\nconfig_ntiedtke_scavchems = true\u003cbr\u003e\n/\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u0026amp;chemistry\u003cbr\u003e\nconfig_gocart2G_do_CA2Gbc       = true\u003cbr\u003e\nconfig_gocart2G_do_CA2Gbr       = true\u003cbr\u003e\nconfig_gocart2G_do_CA2Goc       = true\u003cbr\u003e\nconfig_gocart2G_do_SOA2G        = true\u003cbr\u003e\nconfig_gocart2G_do_DU2G         = true\u003cbr\u003e\nconfig_gocart2G_do_NI2G         = true\u003cbr\u003e\nconfig_gocart2G_do_SS2G         = true\u003cbr\u003e\nconfig_gocart2G_do_SU2G         = true\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003cstrong\u003econfig_gocart2G_toRRTMG = true\u003c/strong\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003cstrong\u003econfig_gocart2G_toMYNN  = true\u003c/strong\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003cstrong\u003econfig_gocart2G_toTHOM = true\u003c/strong\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003cstrong\u003econfig_gocart2G_toNTIEDTKE = true\u003c/strong\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n/\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eA detailed documentation of all the chemistry namelist options will be generated using Registry*.xml files to preserve consistency between the model registry files and documentation.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eWorkflow for preparing a MPAS-GOCART2G simulation\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-workflow-for-preparing-a-mpas-gocart2g-simulation\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Workflow for preparing a 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eFigure 2 displays the workflow for preparing a MPAS-GOCART2G simulation. The lefthand column are detailed in Chapter 1 and the righthand column (boxes outlined in purple) are described in Chapter 2.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\":alt: MPAS-GOCART2G Workflow Diagram\n:align: center\n:width: 100%\n\n**Figure 2.** Workflow for preparing MPAS-GOCART2G simulations. Boxes shaded in blue and red are described in the MPAS-A Tutorial Guide and MPAS-GOCART2G github README.md file, respectively. Boxes shaded in yellow are tasks specific to the GOCART-2G configuration. The gray boxes are MPAS-ready files from the pre-processing steps and the green boxes are the MPAS-GOCART2G executables and output files.\"\u003e\u003cpre lang=\"{figure}\" class=\"notranslate\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003e:alt: MPAS-GOCART2G Workflow Diagram\n:align: center\n:width: 100%\n\n**Figure 2.** Workflow for preparing MPAS-GOCART2G simulations. Boxes shaded in blue and red are described in the MPAS-A Tutorial Guide and MPAS-GOCART2G github README.md file, respectively. Boxes shaded in yellow are tasks specific to the GOCART-2G configuration. The gray boxes are MPAS-ready files from the pre-processing steps and the green boxes are the MPAS-GOCART2G executables and output files.\n\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cdiv octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003ePrerequisites\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-prerequisites\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Prerequisites\" href=\"#prerequisites\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eMPAS-A requires that your Derecho environment is set up with available capabilities, such as MPI-2, NetCDF-4, PnetCDF, and PIO libraries. To learn how to set up your environment, please see the instructions in Chapter 0 of the \u003ca href=\"https://www2.mmm.ucar.edu/projects/mpas/tutorial/Virtual2026/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eMPAS Tutorial - Practice Guide\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eNote: If you are using tcsh instead of bash, the PATH can be set as:\u003cbr\u003e\nsetenv PATH /glade/campaign/mmm/wmr/mpas_tutorial/metis/bin:${PATH}\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eDownload MPAS-GOCART2G from Github:\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-download-mpas-gocart2g-from-github\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Download MPAS-GOCART2G from Github:\" href=\"#download-mpas-gocart2g-from-github\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eFirst, go to a directory where you would like to store the MPAS-GOCART2G source code and processing toos, such as the /glade/work/$USER directory where $USER is your login name on Derecho.\u003cbr\u003e\ncd  /glade/work/$USER\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eDepending on the version and host repository of the model, especially for development branches, you may need to set up an SSH key for the Derecho environment. Instructions for this can be found on this page \u003ca href=\"https://docs.github.com/en/authentication/connecting-to-github-with-ssh/generating-a-new-ssh-key-and-adding-it-to-the-ssh-agent\"\u003ePopulating GitHub SSH Keys and Agents\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eNext, clone the code from Github or copy a version from campaign storage.\u003cbr\u003e\ngit clone \u003ca href=\"mailto:git@github.com\"\u003egit@github.com\u003c/a\u003e:NCAR/MPAS-GOCART2G.git\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eOr copy the code from the ACOM campaign storage location:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003ecp -rp /glade/campaign/acom/MUSICA/MPAS/MPAS-GOCART2G .\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThis will give you all of the tools needed to run MPAS-GOCART2g, including the source code in the \u003cem\u003egocartMPAS\u003c/em\u003e directory. The top level directory should include the following:\u003cbr\u003e\nls -l MPAS_GOCART2G/\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003etotal 20\ndrwxr-xr-x 7 USER ncar 4096 Oct 22 09:43 gocartMPAS\ndrwxr-xr-x 2 USER ncar 4096 Oct 22 09:43 init_MPAS-GOCART2G\n-rw-r--r-- 1 USER ncar 2311 Oct 22 09:43 LICENSE\n-rw-r--r-- 1 USER ncar  252 Oct 22 09:43 README.md\ndrwxr-xr-x 2 USER ncar 4096 Oct 22 09:43 UPTEMPO\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eWhere the source code is located in gocartMPAS. Navigate to this directory and the following should be the directory contents:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003ecd MPAS-GOCART2G/gocartMPAS\nls -l\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003etotal 74\u003cbr\u003e\ndrwxrwxr-x+      4 USER acom-weather 16384 Sep 15 11:33 cmake/\u003cbr\u003e\n-rwxrwxr-x+     1 USER acom-weather  8128 Sep 15 11:33 CMakeLists.txt*\u003cbr\u003e\ndrwxrwxr-x+      3 USER acom-weather 16384 Sep 15 11:33 docs/\u003cbr\u003e\n-rwxrwxr-x+     1 USER acom-weather  3131 Sep 15 11:33 INSTALL*\u003cbr\u003e\n-rwxrwxr-x+     1 USER acom-weather  2311 Sep 15 11:33 LICENSE*\u003cbr\u003e\n-rwxrwxr-x+     1 USER acom-weather 55424 Sep 15 11:33 Makefile*\u003cbr\u003e\n-rwxrwxr-x+     1 USER acom-weather  2811 Sep 15 11:33 README.md*\u003cbr\u003e\ndrwxrwxr-x+     14 USER acom-weather 16384 Sep 15 11:33 src/\u003cbr\u003e\ndrwxrwxr-x+      5 USER acom-weather 16384 Sep 15 11:33 testing_and_setup/\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eCompile MPAS-GOCART2G:\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-compile-mpas-gocart2g\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Compile MPAS-GOCART2G:\" href=\"#compile-mpas-gocart2g\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eBefore starting, make sure you have all of the following in place:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eAccess to the Derecho HPC environment and a valid project account key (\u003ccode\u003e$PROJ\u003c/code\u003e)\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eA working directory on Derecho or another supported HPC system\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eEither a cloned copy of the MPAS-GOCART2G repository or a local copy from campaign storage\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eA valid GitHub SSH key if you are cloning from GitHub (see the instructions in the background section)\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eIf those prerequisties are met, compiling MPAS-GOCART2G follows the same steps as that for MPAS-A compilation. Please follow the steps outlined in chapter 1.2 of the \u003ca href=\"https://www2.mmm.ucar.edu/projects/mpas/tutorial/Virtual2026/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eMPAS Tutorial - Practice Guide\u003c/a\u003e. For those who are familiar with compiling MPAS-A, the commands are the following:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"cd gocartMPAS  \nqcmd -A $PROJ -- make gnu CORE=init_atmosphere GOCART2G=true AUTOCLEAN=true  \nqcmd -A $PROJ -- make gnu CORE=atmosphere GOCART2G=true AUTOCLEAN=true\"\u003e\u003cpre class=\"notranslate\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003ecd gocartMPAS  \nqcmd -A $PROJ -- make gnu CORE=init_atmosphere GOCART2G=true AUTOCLEAN=true  \nqcmd -A $PROJ -- make gnu CORE=atmosphere GOCART2G=true AUTOCLEAN=true\n\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003ewhere $PROJ is the NCAR HPC Derecho project account key that your work is charged to. If you don’t have access to Derecho, please refer to job submission guidelines for your local HPC. If you want to debug your simulation because of an error, you can compile the code with the DEBUG option turned on:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"qcmd -A $PROJ -- make gnu CORE=init_atmosphere GOCART2G=true AUTOCLEAN=true DEBUG=true  \nqcmd -A $PROJ -- make gnu CORE=atmosphere GOCART2G=true AUTOCLEAN=true DEBUG=true\"\u003e\u003cpre class=\"notranslate\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003eqcmd -A $PROJ -- make gnu CORE=init_atmosphere GOCART2G=true AUTOCLEAN=true DEBUG=true  \nqcmd -A $PROJ -- make gnu CORE=atmosphere GOCART2G=true AUTOCLEAN=true DEBUG=true\n\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eWhat a successful compile should produce\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-what-a-successful-compile-should-produce\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: What a successful compile should produce\" href=\"#what-a-successful-compile-should-produce\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eWhen both builds finish successfully, your repository directory should contain the executables, namelist files, and stream files needed to set up and run MPAS-GOCART2G.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eExpected files include:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003einit_atmosphere_model*\u003cbr\u003e\natmosphere_model*\u003cbr\u003e\nbuild_tables*\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003enamelist.init_atmosphere\u003cbr\u003e\nnamelist.atmosphere\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003estream_list.atmosphere.output\u003cbr\u003e\nstreams.atmosphere\u003cbr\u003e\nstreams.init_atmosphere\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eFor more information about these files, see the \u003ca href=\"https://www2.mmm.ucar.edu/projects/mpas/tutorial/Virtual2026/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eMPAS Tutorial - Practice Guide\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eBuild the Thompson lookup tables\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-build-the-thompson-lookup-tables\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Build the Thompson lookup tables\" href=\"#build-the-thompson-lookup-tables\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eThe next step\u003c/strong\u003e is to create the look-up tables used by the Thompson microphysics scheme by issuing the following command. Depending on the version of MPAS-GOCART2G you are using, these table may already be compiled within the model, so check for them in this directory first:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003esrc/core_atmosphere/physics/physics_wrf/files/\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eIf they are not present they must be built using:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e./build_tables\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eIt will take 10-15 minutes to complete this job. Once it is finished, the following files now reside in the directory.\u003cbr\u003e\nMP_THOMPSON_QRacrQG_DATA.DBL\u003cbr\u003e\nMP_THOMPSON_QRacrQS_DATA.DBL\u003cbr\u003e\nMP_THOMPSON_freezeH2O_DATA.DBL\u003cbr\u003e\nMP_THOMPSON_QIautQS_DATA.DBL\u003cbr\u003e\nAs suggested by the program output, copy these files to the src/core_atmosphere/physics/physics_wrf/files/ directory.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThen link to those files from the compile directory (MPAS-GOCART2G/gocartMPAS).\u003cbr\u003e\nln -s src/core_atmosphere/physics/physics_wrf/files/MP_THOMPSON* .\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eCheckpoint: what you should have before moving on\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-checkpoint-what-you-should-have-before-moving-on\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Checkpoint: what you should have before moving on\" href=\"#checkpoint-what-you-should-have-before-moving-on\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eAt this point, you should be able to confirm all of the following:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eThe MPAS-GOCART2G source code is present in \u003ccode\u003egocartMPAS/\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eThe model executables were built successfully\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eThe lookup tables were generated and copied to the physics files directory\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eYou are ready to create and submit a case run directory\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eIf any of these items are missing, go back and finish the compile step before proceeding.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eMPAS-GOCART2G I/O:\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-mpas-gocart2g-io\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: MPAS-GOCART2G I/O:\" href=\"#mpas-gocart2g-io\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eSimilar to MPAS-A, the reading and writing of model fields MPAS-GOCART2G is handled by user-configurable streams. We refer the users to MPAS-A User’s guide to familiarize themselves with these streams (\u003ca href=\"https://www2.mmm.ucar.edu/projects/mpas/site/documentation/users_guide/configuring_io.html\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eMPAS IO\u003c/a\u003e). In addition to the standard MPAS output, the MPAS-GOCART2G I/O requires input streams for anthropogenic, biomass burning, and biogenic emissions and output streams for three-dimensional output of aerosols and related variables, diagnostic variables (e.g., total PM2.5, total and individual species AOD at 550 nm, Angstrom exponent, etc.). Example stream files are provided with the MPAS-GOCART2G release.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eRunning MPAS-GOCART2G: Example 1\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-running-mpas-gocart2g-example-1\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Running MPAS-GOCART2G: Example 1\" href=\"#running-mpas-gocart2g-example-1\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe practice example (Example-1) is a global 60-km uniform grid mesh with a start date of 15 October 2024 at 00:00 UTC. The simulation can be run for 5 days, but for testing whether MPAS-GOCART2G works or not, \u003cstrong\u003ethis example will perform a 3-hour simulation\u003c/strong\u003e which should take about 10 minutes of wallclock time. Note, it is okay to run a 5-day simulation, but be aware of the computational costs. We find that the 5-day simulation takes about 8 hours of wallclock time for the number of CPUs requested in the submission script.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eIt is best to run MPAS_GOCART2G on the Derecho scratch directory. Begin by creating a run directory and moving to that directory.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003emkdir -p /glade/derecho/scratch/$USER/gocartMPAS/Example-1\u003cbr\u003e\ncd /glade/derecho/scratch/$USER/gocartMPAS/Example-1\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eFor this example, we have provided a shell script that will set up and build your simulation directory and the job submission scripts for this example. You can copy this script into your new directory using the following command.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003ecp /glade/campaign/acom/MUSICA/MPAS/scripts/submit_mpas_gocart.bash .\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThis copies a helper script that will set up the case and create the job submission scripts and submit them as dependent jobs based on your case setup. The script is configurable and can be modified for other case studies (for example, the workflow in Chapter 2).\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eIn the script, you will need to change line 11 (\u003ccode\u003eproj=P19010000\u003c/code\u003e) to use the Derecho Account Key that you can use to submit your jobs. If you don’t have access to Derecho, please refer to job submission guidelines for your local HPC.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cem\u003eWhat does the script do?\u003c/em\u003e\nThe script creates a new directory in which the \u003cem\u003einit_atmosphere\u003c/em\u003e and \u003cem\u003eatmosphere\u003c/em\u003e executables are run. The directory’s name is based on the start date of the simulation. In this example, the script creates the directory, 20241015/.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe commands within the script are documented in the file, and the workflow it implements is more structured than the original exercise description. The script performs the following actions in order:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003col dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eSet simulation controls and run metadata: start date, forecast length, grid name, project account, and whether to create the static chemistry file and/or nudging stage.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eDefine the necessary directory paths for the MPAS-GOCART2G source code, the forecast output directory, GFS meteorology, MERRA-2 initialization files, emissions, background oxidant fields, grid files, and the namelist/stream templates.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eCreate the case directory (for example, RUN_001/20241015) and compute the simulation start and end dates used to populate the namelist templates.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eCopy the standard namelist and stream templates from the local input directories, then render them in place by replacing the grid placeholder (\u003ccode\u003e__GRID_NAME__\u003c/code\u003e) and writing the final \u003ccode\u003enamelist.*\u003c/code\u003e and \u003ccode\u003estreams.*\u003c/code\u003e files to the run directory.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eLink the default MPAS grid, static field files, background data, emissions, GFS/MERRA files, and compiled executables (\u003ccode\u003einit_atmosphere_model\u003c/code\u003e, \u003ccode\u003eatmosphere_model\u003c/code\u003e) into the case directory so that the model can run without manual file copying.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003ePrepare and write the PBS job scripts for the required model stages. The default workflow is \u003ccode\u003estatic\u003c/code\u003e init-atmosphere, then \u003ccode\u003echem\u003c/code\u003e init-atmosphere, and then the forecast atmosphere step. If \u003ccode\u003erun_nudging=TRUE\u003c/code\u003e, the script also prepares a \u003ccode\u003enudging\u003c/code\u003e init-atmosphere stage followed by the nudged atmosphere stage.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eSubmit the generated job scripts on Derecho with the project account and core allocation configured in the script. The atmosphere jobs are staged after the required init-atmosphere jobs complete so the case builds in the proper order.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ol\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eReview the script to ensure the project number, start date, forecast length, and paths for the source code and output files (\u003ccode\u003epath_fcst\u003c/code\u003e) are correct. Then submit the script on Derecho:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e./submit_mpas_gocart.bash\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe script automatically creates the files needed for the case setup, including the generated namelists and stream files in the run directory. This is especially useful for new users because it reduces the number of manual edits needed when setting up a case that differs from the tutorial example.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe init_atmosphere code creates the init_chems_emissions.nc file, which contains initial conditions for meteorology, static geographical fields, and all GOCART2G chemical (both gas and aerosol) species as well as emissions from anthropogenic, biomass burning, and biogenic sources. A successful submisison of this script should look like this:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003euser@derecho://glade/derecho/scratch/$USER/gocartMPAS/Example-1\u0026gt; ./submit_mpas_gocart.bash\nThe script is running from: /glade/derecho/scratch/$USER/gocartMPAS/Example-1\nMPAS-GOCART-2G forecast start date is: 2024-10-15_00:00:00\nMPAS-GOCART-2G forecast end date is:   2024-10-15_03:00:00\nSkipping static init_atmosphere step because create_static=FALSE\nPrepared chemistry init_atmosphere run in:\n/glade/derecho/scratch/$USER/gocartMPAS/Example-1/RUN_001/20241015\nSkipping nudging init_atmosphere step because run_nudging=FALSE\nWarning: init_chems_emissions.nc not found in\n/glade/derecho/scratch/$USER/gocartMPAS/Example-1/RUN_001/20241015; chemistry atmosphere run may fail without the chemistry initialization.\nCreate this file by running run_init_chem.pbs.\nPrepared chemistry atmosphere run in: /glade/derecho/scratch/$USER/gocartMPAS/Example-1/RUN_001/20241015\nSkipping run_init_static.pbs (file not found)\nSubmitting root job (held): run_init_chem.pbs\nSkipping run_init_nudging.pbs (file not found)\nSubmitting dependent job: run_atm_chem.pbs (afterok:7965678.desched1)\nSkipping run_atm_nudging.pbs (file not found)\nReleasing chain starting at 7965678.desched1\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eYou can check the status of the submitted using qstat, which should give output similar to this:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003euser@derecho:/glade/derecho/scratch/$USER/gocartMPAS/Example-1\u0026gt; qstat -u $USER\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003edesched1:\nReq'd  Req'd   Elap\nJob ID          Username Queue    Jobname    SessID NDS TSK Memory Time  S Time\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003chr\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e7965679.desche* lacey    cpu      atm_chem      --    1  64  235gb 01:00 H   --\n7965678.desche* lacey    cpu      init_chem     --    1  64  235gb 00:45 Q   --\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eWhen atm_chem starts running and the atmosphere code integrates through the simulation, the following hourly output files are created in the run directory:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e163842.output.2024-10-15_0*.00.00.nc — These files contain selected variables (including aerosol concentrations) specified in “stream_list.output”.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eYou will not generate the 163842.restart.2024-10*.nc files with this example run as the defined restart timestep is 12:00 hours, and the simulation run time is only 3 hours. Restart files are checkpoints of the model state and can be used to restart a simulation from the point they are written.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eChapter 2 (Section 5) discusses the definition of output in the streams file in more detail allowing for more control and flexibility in MPAS-GOCART2G simulations.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eQuick Viewing of the Model Results\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-quick-viewing-of-the-model-results\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Quick Viewing of the Model Results\" href=\"#quick-viewing-of-the-model-results\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eUse ncvis\u003c/strong\u003e\nThe ncvis command is similar to ncview but ncvis can read unstructured grid meshes. The ncvis command can be used to view both input and output files in netcdf format.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eBe sure the ncvis module is loaded in your environment.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003emodule load ncvis\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe ncvis command also needs information about the grid mesh of the output file, which requires an additional argument in the command line.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003encvis grid_filename output_filename\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eSpecifically, for Example 1, the command is\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003encvis x1.163842.grid.nc 163842.output.2024-10-15_03.00.00.nc\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eConvert file to latitude-longitude grid and use ncview\u003c/strong\u003e\nFor viewing model output to confirm that the model is running correctly, we recommend converting the model output to a regular latitude-longitude grid using the \u003cem\u003econvert_mpas\u003c/em\u003e tool. The converted output (on a latitude-longitude grid) can then be viewed using the \u003cem\u003encview\u003c/em\u003e command or with plotting scripts.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eSection 3.4 of the \u003ca href=\"https://www2.mmm.ucar.edu/projects/mpas/tutorial/Virtual2026/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eMPAS Tutorial - Practice Guide\u003c/a\u003e) describes how to obtain the \u003cem\u003econvert_mpas\u003c/em\u003e tool and compile it. An example command line for running \u003cem\u003econvert_mpas\u003c/em\u003e (we recommend adding the full path of convert_mpas to your $PATH so that you can use it in any directory) is given here.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003econvert_mpas x1.163842.static_chems.nc 163842.output.2024-10-15_03.00.00.nc\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eWhen \u003cem\u003econvert_mpas\u003c/em\u003e is run, it creates a file called \u003cem\u003elatlon.nc\u003c/em\u003e. Note, if you wish to convert other MPAS-GOCART2G files to a latitude-longitude grid, then you must rename or delete the current latlon.nc file.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eMore information about plotting MPAS-GOCART2G output is given in \u003cstrong\u003eAppendix B\u003c/strong\u003e. Specifically, we recommend using the uxarray within python to plot results on the native grid mesh.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eChapter 2 Setting Up a Different Case\u003c/strong\u003e  {#chapter-2-setting-up-a-different-case}\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-chapter-2-setting-up-a-different-case--chapter-2-setting-up-a-different-case\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Chapter 2 Setting Up a Different Case  {#chapter-2-setting-up-a-different-case}\" href=\"#chapter-2-setting-up-a-different-case--chapter-2-setting-up-a-different-case\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eIntroduction\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-introduction\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Introduction\" href=\"#introduction\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThis chapter provides information on setting up a different case using the same grid mesh as in chapter 1 but running a different time period. A description is given on where to obtain input files for running the program \u003cem\u003einit_atmosphere\u003c/em\u003e which sets up the MPAS-GOCART2G simulation. The chapter is structured by the four types of input files needed to run a new case:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003col dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eMeteorology fields\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eMERRA-2 files for initial chemical conditions\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eEmissions files (anthropogenic, biogenic, and biomass burning)\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eBackground oxidant and aerosol optical-property data\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ol\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eIf any one of these is missing or incorrectly formatted, the model setup will not be complete.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eBefore you begin: quick checklist\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-before-you-begin-quick-checklist\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Before you begin: quick checklist\" href=\"#before-you-begin-quick-checklist\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eBefore moving into the detailed steps, confirm that you have:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003ea valid Derecho project account (\u003ccode\u003e$PROJ\u003c/code\u003e), if using Derecho\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003ethe MPAS-GOCART2G source code compiled and available\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003ea working directory for pre-processing files\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eaccess to the required input datasets from MERRA-2, CAMS, FINN, and the MPAS campaign directories\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eAt the end of this chapter, the goal is to have all required inputs staged and ready so that the \u003ccode\u003einit_atmosphere\u003c/code\u003e setup step can run cleanly.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e1. Preparation of Meteorology Fields\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-1-preparation-of-meteorology-fields\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 1. Preparation of Meteorology Fields\" href=\"#1-preparation-of-meteorology-fields\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eA 2-week spin-up is needed before the start of your case study, so you should obtain meteorology input data beginning 2 weeks before the case start date. The end date should match the end date of the case study.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eFor a first case, it is strongly recommended that you use the same 60-km uniform grid mesh as Chapter 1. Once you are comfortable with the setup, you can experiment with other mesh configurations.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe MPAS-A tutorial contains the detailed instructions for generating the required meteorology input files. In practice, you should follow the tutorial’s preparation steps to create a \u003ccode\u003emet_data/\u003c/code\u003e directory containing files such as:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"GFS:yyyy-mm-dd-hh\nERA5:yyyy-mm-dd-hh\"\u003e\u003cpre lang=\"text\" class=\"notranslate\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003eGFS:yyyy-mm-dd-hh\nERA5:yyyy-mm-dd-hh\n\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThese files are then converted into the intermediate file format expected by \u003ccode\u003einit_atmosphere_model\u003c/code\u003e. If you are planning on runnijng using meteorlogical nidging, you will need to generate these files at the apporpirate timesteps (usually 6 hours) for the entire simulation period.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eExpected outcome: after this step, you should have a complete \u003ccode\u003emet_data/\u003c/code\u003e directory covering the necessary spin-up and simulation window.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e2. Getting MERRA data and convert to MPAS intermediate files\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-2-getting-merra-data-and-convert-to-mpas-intermediate-files\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 2. Getting MERRA data and convert to MPAS intermediate files\" href=\"#2-getting-merra-data-and-convert-to-mpas-intermediate-files\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eMERRA2 files provide initial concentrations for the predicted fields as well as HNO3, NH3, CO, and isoprene. The HNO3 and NH3 are used for the thermodynamics calculation of the SO4-NH4-NO3 system. Isoprene and CO are used for the simplified SOA production.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eAppendix A provides information on how to download MERRA data from the NASA Earthdata site. You will need to have an NASA Earthdata user name and password to download the data when running the python script.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eOnce the MERRA input files are in a local directory, converting the MERRA data to the MPAS-A grid mesh can be done with the python MERRA processing script, \u003cem\u003erun_processing.py\u003c/em\u003e. The script and instructions for running the script can be found on the \u003ca href=\"https://github.com/NCAR_MPAS-GOCART2G/init_MPAS-GOCART2G\"\u003einit_MPAS_GOCART2G\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eExpected outcome: after this step, you should have the chemically prepared MERRA-2 intermediate files in the correct format for the MPAS grid.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e3. Preparation of the Emissions via UPTEMPO\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-3-preparation-of-the-emissions-via-uptempo\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 3. Preparation of the Emissions via UPTEMPO\" href=\"#3-preparation-of-the-emissions-via-uptempo\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe emissions preprocessor, UPTEMPO, is a Python tool used to prepare the anthropogenic, biogenic, and biomass burning emissions needed for MPAS-GOCART2G.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eYou can obtain UPTEMPO from the MPAS-GOCART2G repository, or clone it directly with:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003egit clone \u003ca href=\"https://github.com/NCAR/MPAS-GOCART2G/UPTEMPO\"\u003ehttps://github.com/NCAR/MPAS-GOCART2G/UPTEMPO\u003c/a\u003e UPTEMPO\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eTo run UPTEMPO you can either use your Terminal or submit a job to Derecho’s PBS system.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eGo to the \u003ca href=\"https://github.com/NCAR/MPAS-GOCART2G/UPTEMPO\"\u003eUPTEMPO\u003c/a\u003e and follow the instructions in the README files.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch4 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eAnthropogenic emissions from CAMS\u003c/h4\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-anthropogenic-emissions-from-cams\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Anthropogenic emissions from CAMS\" href=\"#anthropogenic-emissions-from-cams\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eUse the instructions in \u003ccode\u003eREADME_CAMS.md\u003c/code\u003e and the configuration file \u003ccode\u003econfig_cams_anth_regrid.yaml\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThis file includes the CAMS v6.2 emissions directories and the MPAS uniform 60-km grid configuration. If the required year is not already available on Derecho, download the needed files from the ECCAD database. See the \u003ca href=\"https://eccad.aeris-data.fr/user_guide/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eECCAD Users Guide\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eImportant: download all sectors. GOCART-2G requires sector-resolved emissions for different species.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch4 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eBiogenic emissions from CAMS\u003c/h4\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-biogenic-emissions-from-cams\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Biogenic emissions from CAMS\" href=\"#biogenic-emissions-from-cams\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eUse \u003ccode\u003eREADME_CAMS_BIOG.md\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eBiogenic emissions are based on a climatological dataset, so a specific year is not required. The example setup uses 2019 as a placeholder; the resulting fields are climatological and do not represent a specific year.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch4 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eBiomass burning emissions from FINN2.5\u003c/h4\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-biomass-burning-emissions-from-finn25\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Biomass burning emissions from FINN2.5\" href=\"#biomass-burning-emissions-from-finn25\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eUse \u003ccode\u003eREADME_FINN.md\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eTo obtain FINN v2.5 data:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eFor 2012-2023, use the data from the Geoscience Data Exchange (GDEX) archive: \u003ca href=\"https://gdex.ucar.edu/datasets/d312009/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eGDEX Fire Inventory from NCAR database\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eFor 2024-present, use the near-real-time daily files from \u003ca href=\"https://www.acom.ucar.edu/acresp/MODELING/finn_emis_txt/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eFINN Emission text files\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe data file type depends on the period:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eannual files: use \u003ccode\u003efile_type: 'annual'\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003edaily files: use \u003ccode\u003efile_type: 'daily'\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eAfter downloading the files, copy them to a local working directory and decompress them as needed using \u003ccode\u003egunzip\u003c/code\u003e on Linux.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eExpected outcome: after this step, you should have processed anthropogenic, biogenic, and biomass-burning emission files ready to be linked into the case run directory.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e4. Background Oxidants Data and Aerosol Optical Properties Data\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-4-background-oxidants-data-and-aerosol-optical-properties-data\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 4. Background Oxidants Data and Aerosol Optical Properties Data\" href=\"#4-background-oxidants-data-and-aerosol-optical-properties-data\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eBackground oxidant data is currently produced using climatological GMI simulations. These background oxidant data files are located in the following directory:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e/glade/campaign/acom/MUSICA/MPAS/input/BACKGROUND\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe \u003cem\u003esubmit_mpas_gocart.bash\u003c/em\u003e script links to this directory, so no changes by the user are needed.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eAerosol optical properties are from Chin et al., 2002. These data files are located in the following directory:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e/glade/campaign/acom/MUSICA/MPAS/input/optics_files\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eAgain, the example submission script links here automatically.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eIf you would like to use different background or aerosol optical property data files, then simply create those files in the same format as those provided in the directory and modify the \u003cem\u003esubmit_mpas_gocart.bash\u003c/em\u003e script to link to the new files.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cem\u003eNote:\u003c/em\u003e In the future, obtaining the background oxidants data from WACCM output will become available.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eCheckpoint before setting up the case\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-checkpoint-before-setting-up-the-case\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Checkpoint before setting up the case\" href=\"#checkpoint-before-setting-up-the-case\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eAt this point, you should have the following prepared and ready:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003emeteorology files covering the spin-up and simulation period\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eMERRA-2 files converted to MPAS-compatible format\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eprocessed emissions files from UPTEMPO\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003ebackground oxidant and optical property input files\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eOnce these are all in place, the next steps are to modify \u003ccode\u003estreams.init_atmosphere\u003c/code\u003e and \u003ccode\u003estreams.atmosphere\u003c/code\u003e, then create and submit the new case.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e5. Modification of streams.init_atmosphere and streams.atmosphere\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-5-modification-of-streamsinit_atmosphere-and-streamsatmosphere\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 5. Modification of streams.init_atmosphere and streams.atmosphere\" href=\"#5-modification-of-streamsinit_atmosphere-and-streamsatmosphere\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eFor a new case, you usually do not edit the compiled \u003ccode\u003estreams.*\u003c/code\u003e and \u003ccode\u003enamelist.*\u003c/code\u003e files in the source tree. Instead, the run script generates a case-specific copy from templates stored in the model input directories. This is especially important now that the example \u003ccode\u003esubmit_mpas_gocart.bash\u003c/code\u003e script generates these files automatically from template files and substitutes the case metadata (for example, the grid name via \u003ccode\u003e__GRID_NAME__\u003c/code\u003e).\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe typical workflow is:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003col dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eStart from the template files in the model source tree.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eCopy them into the case run directory.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eReplace placeholders such as \u003ccode\u003e__GRID_NAME__\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eUpdate any file names, emission stream names, or output selections for the new case.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eRun the generated \u003ccode\u003einit_atmosphere\u003c/code\u003e or \u003ccode\u003eatmosphere\u003c/code\u003e job using the case-specific \u003ccode\u003enamelist.*\u003c/code\u003e and \u003ccode\u003estreams.*\u003c/code\u003e files.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ol\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThis is the recommended approach because it keeps the setup reproducible and avoids editing the original compiled templates by hand.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eFor more information about what these files contain and how they are used, see \u003ca href=\"https://www2.mmm.ucar.edu/projects/mpas/site/documentation/users_guide/configuring_io.html\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eChapter 5 of the MPAS User’s Guide\u003c/a\u003e. For MPAS-GOCART2G, the essential point is that the model needs stream definitions for anthropogenic, biomass-burning, and biogenic emissions, plus the standard output variables you want to save.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eWhat to check in a new case\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-what-to-check-in-a-new-case\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: What to check in a new case\" href=\"#what-to-check-in-a-new-case\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eWhen you create a new case, the emission file names in the stream files often differ from the default template values. Therefore, after the script creates the case-specific files, review \u003ccode\u003estreams.atmosphere\u003c/code\u003e and \u003ccode\u003estreams.init_atmosphere\u003c/code\u003e and confirm that the file paths and stream names match the files generated in the emissions preprocessing step.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe simplest workflow is:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eedit the templates before submitting the case if the change is systematic, or\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eedit the generated case files in the run directory after the script has created them if the change is case specific.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe example submission script is designed to link the stream files correctly and generate the matching PBS job files. It calls commands like:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"mpiexec ./init_atmosphere_model -n \u0026lt;namelist\u0026gt; -s \u0026lt;stream\u0026gt;\nmpiexec ./atmosphere_model -n \u0026lt;namelist\u0026gt; -s \u0026lt;stream\u0026gt;\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003empiexec ./init_atmosphere_model -n \u003cspan class=\"pl-k\"\u003e\u0026lt;\u003c/span\u003enamelist\u003cspan class=\"pl-k\"\u003e\u0026gt;\u003c/span\u003e -s \u003cspan class=\"pl-k\"\u003e\u0026lt;\u003c/span\u003estream\u003cspan class=\"pl-k\"\u003e\u0026gt;\u003c/span\u003e\nmpiexec ./atmosphere_model -n \u003cspan class=\"pl-k\"\u003e\u0026lt;\u003c/span\u003enamelist\u003cspan class=\"pl-k\"\u003e\u0026gt;\u003c/span\u003e -s \u003cspan class=\"pl-k\"\u003e\u0026lt;\u003c/span\u003estream\u003cspan class=\"pl-k\"\u003e\u0026gt;\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThis ensures the namelist, stream, and job submission files stay consistent for each model stage.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eAdding a custom output stream\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-adding-a-custom-output-stream\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Adding a custom output stream\" href=\"#adding-a-custom-output-stream\"\u003e\u003csvg 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eFor user-defined output, follow the instructions in Section 6.2 of the \u003ca href=\"https://www2.mmm.ucar.edu/projects/mpas/tutorial/Virtual2026/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eMPAS Tutorial - Practice Guide\u003c/a\u003e. In short, output streams can be defined either by listing variables directly in the stream block or by referencing a separate \u003ccode\u003estream_list\u003c/code\u003e
