class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\n        \u003cspan class=\"pl-e\"\u003eoutput_interval\u003c/span\u003e=\u003cspan class=\"pl-s\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003e00_01:00:00\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u0026gt;\n    \u0026lt;\u003cspan class=\"pl-ent\"\u003efile\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan class=\"pl-e\"\u003ename\u003c/span\u003e=\u003cspan class=\"pl-s\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003estream_list.sfc_winds\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e/\u0026gt;\n\u0026lt;/\u003cspan class=\"pl-ent\"\u003estream\u003c/span\u003e\u0026gt;\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThis is the same stream definition but it uses a text file to list the output variables instead of writing each variable directly in the XML block.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eGenerated \u003ccode\u003estream_list.*\u003c/code\u003e files\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-generated-stream_list-files\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Generated stream_list.* files\" href=\"#generated-stream_list-files\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eWhen MPAS-GOCART2G is compiled, a number of \u003ccode\u003estream_list.atmosphere.*\u003c/code\u003e files are created. The standard files are:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"stream_list.atmosphere.diagnostics\nstream_list.atmosphere.output\nstream_list.atmosphere.surface\"\u003e\u003cpre lang=\"text\" class=\"notranslate\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003estream_list.atmosphere.diagnostics\nstream_list.atmosphere.output\nstream_list.atmosphere.surface\n\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThese files define which diagnostic and output variables are written during the simulation. For a new case, review these files after the script has generated the case directory to confirm that the correct output variables and emission streams are in place.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eIf you are following the template-driven workflow in the \u003ccode\u003esubmit_mpas_gocart.bash\u003c/code\u003e this is usually the point at which you should check the generated files rather than editing the source templates directly or add in new user-defined stream_list files and fields.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eAppendix A:  Downloading MERRA2 Files\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-appendix-a--downloading-merra2-files\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Appendix A:  Downloading MERRA2 Files\" href=\"#appendix-a--downloading-merra2-files\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThis section describes the procedure for (1) downloading, (2) preparing, and (3) generating the MPAS required MERRA2 fields for GOCART simulations using aerosol and nitrate precursor concentrations.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eNote: To accommodate the need for a 2-week spin-up of the gases and aerosols for MPAS-GOCART2G simulations, be sure to obtain and process MERRA data for the spin-up period as well as the simulation time frame.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eTwo different sets of MERRA2 files are needed to populate the chemical fields needed for GOCART, this first being for aerosol species “inst3_3d_aer_Nv” and the second being for the gaseous species “inst0_3d_ovp_Nv”.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eTo download the correct files, the first step is to copy the following shell script into a temporary location with adequate storage capacity for processing (~15 Gb per day needed), for the purposes of this example we will assume this is completed on Derecho’s scratch directory:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003emkdir /glade/derecho/scratch/$USER/MERRA_TEST/\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003ecd /glade/derecho/scratch/$USER/MERRA_TEST/\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003emkdir SAT_FILES\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003ecd SAT_FILES\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eNext copy the download script from the MPAS campaign storage:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003ecp /glade/campaign/acom/MUSICA/MPAS/input/MERRA2/download_merra.sh .\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eModify the download script starting on line 98 to replace the list of files you will need corresponding to your simulation time. You can do this manually by modifying the file or by generating a list of the files from Earthdata. Make sure to have both the “aer” and “ovp” files for the correct time listed. (NOTE: MERRA2 “ovp” files are not available after 2019 due to shifts in assimilation products. While we are working on a long term fix using other NASA products, for simulations after 2019, please use the 2019 files and a two-week spinup if needed).\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eHere is an example section of the script targeting five days in October 2019 that you can should edit for downloading:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003efetch_urls \u0026lt;\u0026lt;'EDSCEOF'\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003ehttps://data.gesdisc.earthdata.nasa.gov/data/MERRA2/M2I3NVAER.5.12.4/2019/10/MERRA2_400.inst3_3d_aer_Nv.20191015.nc4\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003ehttps://data.gesdisc.earthdata.nasa.gov/data/MERRA2/M2I3NVAER.5.12.4/2019/10/MERRA2_400.inst3_3d_aer_Nv.20191016.nc4\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003ehttps://data.gesdisc.earthdata.nasa.gov/data/MERRA2/M2I3NVAER.5.12.4/2019/10/MERRA2_400.inst3_3d_aer_Nv.20191017.nc4\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003ehttps://data.gesdisc.earthdata.nasa.gov/data/MERRA2/M2I3NVAER.5.12.4/2019/10/MERRA2_400.inst3_3d_aer_Nv.20191018.nc4\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003ehttps://data.gesdisc.earthdata.nasa.gov/data/MERRA2/M2I3NVAER.5.12.4/2019/10/MERRA2_400.inst3_3d_aer_Nv.20191019.nc4\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003ehttps://portal.nccs.nasa.gov/datashare/merra2_gmi/Y2019/M10/MERRA2_GMI.inst0_3d_ovp_Nv.20191015_1200z.nc4\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003ehttps://portal.nccs.nasa.gov/datashare/merra2_gmi/Y2019/M10/MERRA2_GMI.inst0_3d_ovp_Nv.20191016_1200z.nc4\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003ehttps://portal.nccs.nasa.gov/datashare/merra2_gmi/Y2019/M10/MERRA2_GMI.inst0_3d_ovp_Nv.20191017_1200z.nc4\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003ehttps://portal.nccs.nasa.gov/datashare/merra2_gmi/Y2019/M10/MERRA2_GMI.inst0_3d_ovp_Nv.20191018_1200z.nc4\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003ehttps://portal.nccs.nasa.gov/datashare/merra2_gmi/Y2019/M10/MERRA2_GMI.inst0_3d_ovp_Nv.20191019_1200z.nc4\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003eEDSCEOF\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eWhile you can manually update the dates, if you are a more experienced user of the NASA DAACs you can also use one of the subsetting tools to generate the download script or list of URLs to paste in here too. You will now run each script to download the files by running the script using:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003e./download_merra.sh\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eAll of the fields needed for MPAS-GOCART2G are listed in the MERRA_IC config.yaml file in the ‘target’ fields and listed below:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003especies_map:\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"BCPHILIC\", file: \"prefix_in_1\", target: \"BCPHILIC\", desc: \"Hydrophilic Black Carbon Mixing Ratio\", units: \"kg kg^-1\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"BCPHOBIC\", file: \"prefix_in_1\", target: \"BCPHOBIC\", desc: \"Hydrophobic Black Carbon Mixing Ratio\", units: \"kg kg^-1\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"OCPHILIC\", file: \"prefix_in_1\", target: \"OCPHILIC\", desc: \"Hydrophilic Organic Carbon Mixing Ratio\", units: \"kg kg^-1\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"OCPHOBIC\", file: \"prefix_in_1\", target: \"OCPHOBIC\", desc: \"Hydrophobic Organic Carbon Mixing Ratio\", units: \"kg kg^-1\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"DU001\",    file: \"prefix_in_1\", target: \"DU001\",    desc: \"Dust Mixing Ratio in Bin 001\", units: \"kg kg^-1\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"DU002\",    file: \"prefix_in_1\", target: \"DU002\",    desc: \"Dust Mixing Ratio in Bin 002\", units: \"kg kg^-1\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"DU003\",    file: \"prefix_in_1\", target: \"DU003\",    desc: \"Dust Mixing Ratio in Bin 003\", units: \"kg kg^-1\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"DU004\",    file: \"prefix_in_1\", target: \"DU004\",    desc: \"Dust Mixing Ratio in Bin 004\", units: \"kg kg^-1\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"DU005\",    file: \"prefix_in_1\", target: \"DU005\",    desc: \"Dust Mixing Ratio in Bin 005\", units: \"kg kg^-1\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"NI001\",    file: \"prefix_in_1\", target: \"NI001\",    desc: \"Nitrate Mixing Ratio in Bin 001\", units: \"kg kg^-1\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"NI002\",    file: \"prefix_in_1\", target: \"NI002\",    desc: \"Nitrate Mixing Ratio in Bin 002\", units: \"kg kg^-1\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"NI003\",    file: \"prefix_in_1\", target: \"NI003\",    desc: \"Nitrate Mixing Ratio in Bin 003\", units: \"kg kg^-1\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"SO2\",      file: \"prefix_in_1\", target: \"SO2\",      desc: \"Sulphur Dioxide Mixing Ratio\", units: \"kg kg^-1\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"SO2v\",     file: \"prefix_in_1\", target: \"SO2V\",     desc: \"Sulphur Dioxide Mixing Ratio (volcanic)\", units: \"kg kg^-1\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"SO4\",      file: \"prefix_in_1\", target: \"SO4\",      desc: \"Sulfate Mixing Ratio\", units: \"kg kg^-1\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"SO4v\",     file: \"prefix_in_1\", target: \"SO4V\",     desc: \"Sulfate Mixing Ratio (volcanic)\", units: \"kg kg^-1\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"SS001\",    file: \"prefix_in_1\", target: \"SS001\",    desc: \"Seasalt Mixing Ratio in Bin 001\", units: \"kg kg^-1\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"SS002\",    file: \"prefix_in_1\", target: \"SS002\",    desc: \"Seasalt Mixing Ratio in Bin 002\", units: \"kg kg^-1\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"SS003\",    file: \"prefix_in_1\", target: \"SS003\",    desc: \"Seasalt Mixing Ratio in Bin 003\", units: \"kg kg^-1\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"SS004\",    file: \"prefix_in_1\", target: \"SS004\",    desc: \"Seasalt Mixing Ratio in Bin 004\", units: \"kg kg^-1\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"SS005\",    file: \"prefix_in_1\", target: \"SS005\",    desc: \"Seasalt Mixing Ratio in Bin 005\", units: \"kg kg^-1\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"DMS\",      file: \"prefix_in_1\", target: \"DMS\",      desc: \"Dimethylsulphide Mixing Ratio\", units: \"kg kg^-1\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"MSA\",      file: \"prefix_in_1\", target: \"MSA\",      desc: \"Methanesulphonic Acid Mixing Ratio\", units: \"kg kg^-1\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"AIRDENS\",  file: \"prefix_in_1\", target: \"AIRDENS\",  desc: \"Moist_Air_Density\", units: \"kg m^-3\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"RH\",       file: \"prefix_in_1\", target: \"RH\",       desc: \"Relative Humidity\", units: \"-\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"DELP\",     file: \"prefix_in_1\", target: \"DPRES\",    desc: \"Pressure Thickness\", units: \"Pa\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"PS\",       file: \"prefix_in_1\", target: \"PS\",       desc: \"Surface Pressure\", units: \"Pa\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"LWI\",      file: \"prefix_in_1\", target: \"LWI\",      desc: \"Land(1)_Water(0)_Ice(2)_Flag\", units: \"-\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"OVP14_HNO3\",            file: \"prefix_in_2\", target: \"HNO3\",          desc: \"Nitric Acid\", units: \"mole mole-1\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"OVP14_HNO3COND\",        file: \"prefix_in_2\", target: \"HNO3COND\",      desc: \"Condensed Nitric Acid\", units: \"mole mole-1\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"OVP14_GOCART_NH3_VAR\",  file: \"prefix_in_2\", target: \"NH3\",           desc: \"Ammonia\", units: \"mole mole-1\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"OVP14_CO\",              file: \"prefix_in_2\", target: \"CO\",            desc: \"Carboin Monoxide\", units: \"mole mole-1\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003e- {source: \"OVP14_ISOP\",            file: \"prefix_in_2\", target: \"ISOPRENE\",      desc: \"Isoprene\", units: \"mole mole-1\"}\u003c/code\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003ccode\u003eYou can confirm the species mapping between the files using ncdump. Any missing species will be filled in with zeros in the MERRA_IC code, but it is better to fill the data with an available year (2019 has all species available).\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eAppendix B: Visualization of MPAS output\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-appendix-b-visualization-of-mpas-output\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Appendix B: Visualization of MPAS output\" href=\"#appendix-b-visualization-of-mpas-output\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eMPAS-GOCART2G uses a variable-resolution, unstructured spherical centroidal Voronoi mesh for simulation and therefore traditional lat/lon visualization methods (i.e. NCVIEW) do not work with input and output files. Therefore simplified tools have been developed for quick visualization of MPAS files.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eMPAS-A tutorial python script\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-mpas-a-tutorial-python-script\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: MPAS-A tutorial python script\" href=\"#mpas-a-tutorial-python-script\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eThe first method\u003c/strong\u003e is explained in detail in Section 6.5 of the \u003ca href=\"https://www2.mmm.ucar.edu/projects/mpas/tutorial/Virtual2026/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eMPAS Tutorial - Practice Guide\u003c/a\u003e and uses a python script within the terminal to create images that visualize the native MPAS files.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eTo use this method for a file in your run directory first run:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003ecp /glade/campaign/mmm/wmr/mpas_tutorial/python_scripts/latlon_cells.py .\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eto copy the python script to your run directory. This script takes an MPAS file with appropriate grid information (usually the init_chems) and uses that to plot an output netcdf file into a PNG file. To run this you need to input both the grid file and the desired output file, for which this example will be the output init_chems_emissions.nc file.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eln -s /glade/campaign/acom/MUSICA/MPAS/input/\u003ca href=\"http://x1.163842.grid.nc\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003ex1.163842.grid.nc\u003c/a\u003e .\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eTo plot specific outputs you will also need to change the latlon_cells.py file to look at the correct variable. So modify the following line 4 from:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003efield = 'wind_speed_level1_max'\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eto:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003efield = 'bc_anth_less100m'\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eYou can then run the python script using:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003e./latlon_cells.py \u003ca href=\"http://x1.163842.grid.nc\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003ex1.163842.grid.nc\u003c/a\u003e init_chems_emissions.nc\u003c/strong\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003cstrong\u003edisplay cell_plot.png\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eTo output and display the following graphic.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\":alt: Sample Global Emissions Plot\n:align: center\n:width: 100%\n\n**Figure 3.** Global 60km Resolution Surface BC Emissions Example Plot.\"\u003e\u003cpre lang=\"{figure}\" class=\"notranslate\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003e:alt: Sample Global Emissions Plot\n:align: center\n:width: 100%\n\n**Figure 3.** Global 60km Resolution Surface BC Emissions Example Plot.\n\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe default version of the tool works directly with 2D fields, but if you would like to plot 3D variables you will also need to change line 50 from:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003efld = uxds_mpas[field].isel(Time=0)\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eto:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003efld = uxds_mpas[field].isel(Time=0, nVertLevels=0)\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003ewith other options such as zooming in on particular regions discussed in section 6.5 of the \u003ca href=\"https://www2.mmm.ucar.edu/projects/mpas/tutorial/Virtual2026/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eMPAS Tutorial - Practice Guide\u003c/a\u003e\u003cstrong\u003e.\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eRegrid output to a lat-lon file and then view\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-regrid-output-to-a-lat-lon-file-and-then-view\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Regrid output to a lat-lon file and then view\" href=\"#regrid-output-to-a-lat-lon-file-and-then-view\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eThe second method\u003c/strong\u003e works well for quick visualization and those that are most familiar with using ncview and other fixed-grid processes for data analysis. This method is discussed in detail in Section 3.4 and 4.3 of the MPAS-A Tutorial and uses fortran processing (convert_mpas) to convert MPAS-grid files to a fixed latitude and longitude file.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe acquisition and compiling of this tool is described in detail in the tutorial, but the simplified steps are shown here as well for a new installation and use if following the instructions here:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003emkdir /glade/work/$USER/gocartMPAS/mpas_tools\u003c/strong\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003cstrong\u003ecd  /glade/work/$USER/gocartMPAS/mpas_tools\u003c/strong\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003cstrong\u003egit clone \u003ca href=\"https://github.com/mgduda/convert%5C_mpas.git\"\u003ehttps://github.com/mgduda/convert\\_mpas.git\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/strong\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003cstrong\u003ecd convert_mpas\u003c/strong\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003cstrong\u003emake\u003c/strong\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003cstrong\u003eexport PATH=/glade/work/$USER/gocartMPAS/mpas_tools/convert_mpas:${PATH}\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThese steps will give you a compiled version of the convert_mpas tool that you can access and run. Once compiled, you can directly add the last line to your .bashrc file and it will be available automatically each time you log on to Derecho.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe base version of this tool takes an MPAS-gridded file and converts it to a global 0.5 by 0.5 degree netcdf named latlon.nc. If the steps above have been followed, you can simply run the tool targeting the same file above using:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003econvert_mpas init_chems_emissions.nc\u003c/strong\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\nWhich will give you an output file of latlon.nc which has all of the tracers stored in the parent file, just now in the 0.5 by 0.5 degree fixed grid format. This file can now be viewed and plotted using traditional methods using Python or ncview. This tool does not do mass-conservative regridding, so should only be used for reference and data visualization, more complex methods should be used for analysis and generation of publication-ready figures.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eJupyterLab notebook Python script\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-jupyterlab-notebook-python-script\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: JupyterLab notebook Python script\" href=\"#jupyterlab-notebook-python-script\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eThe final method\u003c/strong\u003e is using JupyterLab notebooks and provides the most interactive plotting method. To use this method, users should be comfortable running JupyterHub or JupyterLab on the Derecho or home environments, and more detail can be found here for the NCAR Derecho environment through OnDemand:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://ncar-hpc-docs.readthedocs.io/en/latest/compute-systems/ood/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eNCAR CISL OnDemand\u003c/a\u003e)\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eWithin a new job started in JupyterHub you should create a new workbook using the NPL-2025b environment and paste the following code into the first block:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eimport netCDF4 as nc\u003cbr\u003e\nimport numpy as np\u003cbr\u003e\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\u003cbr\u003e\nimport uxarray as ux\u003cbr\u003e\nimport geoviews.feature as gf\u003cbr\u003e\nimport holoviews as hv\u003cbr\u003e\nimport cartopy.crs as ccrs\u003cbr\u003e\nimport cartopy.feature as cfeature\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003egrid_path = '/glade/campaign/acom/MUSICA/MPAS/input/x1.163842.grid.nc'\u003cbr\u003e\nstat_path = '/glade/campaign/acom/MUSICA/MPAS/input/Example-1/EMIS/x1.163842-2024-anth_black-carbon.MPAS.nc'\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003euxds = ux.open_dataset(grid_path, stat_path, decode_times=False)\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003euxds[\"bc_anth_sum\"].isel(Time=0).plot(\u003cbr\u003e\nheight=800,\u003cbr\u003e\nwidth=1500,\u003cbr\u003e\nclim=(0, 1e-11),\u003cbr\u003e\nclabel=\"kg m$^{-2}$ sec$^{-1}$\",\u003cbr\u003e\nbackend=\"matplotlib\",\u003cbr\u003e\ncmap=\"gist_heat_r\",\u003cbr\u003e\nprojection=ccrs.PlateCarree(),\u003cbr\u003e\nfeatures=[\"borders\", \"coastline\"],\u003cbr\u003e\ntitle=f\"CAMS ANTH EMS: BC\",\u003cbr\u003e\n)\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThis code will ensure the appropriate packages are available and plot one of the MPAS-GOCART2G input emission files, resulting in the following figure in a Holoview frame:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\":alt: Global CAMS BC Emissions\n:align: center\n:width: 100%\n\n**Figure 4.** Global 60km CAMS_ANTH_BC Emissions. \"\u003e\u003cpre lang=\"{figure}\" class=\"notranslate\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003e:alt: Global CAMS BC Emissions\n:align: center\n:width: 100%\n\n**Figure 4.** Global 60km CAMS_ANTH_BC Emissions. \n\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eModification of this code follows most standard matplotlib options and you can change plot location, scales, labels and colormaps easily. As one example, here is another block of code that uses the jet colormap and zooms in over India:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003euxds[\"bc_anth_sum\"].isel(Time=0).plot(\u003cbr\u003e\nheight=800,\u003cbr\u003e\nwidth=1500,\u003cbr\u003e\nclim=(0, 5e-11),\u003cbr\u003e\nclabel=\"kg m$^{-2}$ sec$^{-1}$\",\u003cbr\u003e\nbackend=\"matplotlib\",\u003cbr\u003e\ncmap=\"jet\",\u003cbr\u003e\nprojection=ccrs.PlateCarree(),\u003cbr\u003e\nfeatures=[\"borders\", \"coastline\"],\u003cbr\u003e\ntitle=f\"CAMS ANTH EMS: BC - India Zoom\",\u003cbr\u003e\n# Add these two lines to set the spatial window:\u003cbr\u003e\nxlim=(68, 98),  # Longitude bounds\u003cbr\u003e\nylim=(6, 36),   # Latitude bounds\u003cbr\u003e\n)\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eResulting in the following plot:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\":alt: CAMS BC Emissions over India\n:align: center\n:width: 100%\n\n**Figure 5.** Global 60km CAMS_ANTH_BC Emissions Zoomed in over India.\"\u003e\u003cpre lang=\"{figure}\" class=\"notranslate\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003e:alt: CAMS BC Emissions over India\n:align: center\n:width: 100%\n\n**Figure 5.** Global 60km CAMS_ANTH_BC Emissions Zoomed in over India.\n\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThree dimensional fields can be plotted as well. Changing to the output file of a simulation, hydrophobic black carbon in the lowest model level is plotted below.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003estat_path = '/glade/derecho/scratch/barthm/gocartMPAS/DC3simulations/20120501/163842.output.2012-05-01_03.00.00.nc'\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003euxds = ux.open_dataset(grid_path, stat_path, decode_times=False)\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003euxds[\"qbcphobic\"].isel(Time=0,nVertLevels=0).plot(\u003cbr\u003e\nheight=800,\u003cbr\u003e\nwidth=1500,\u003cbr\u003e\nclim=(0, 1e-9),\u003cbr\u003e\nclabel=\"kg kg$^{-1}$\",\u003cbr\u003e\nbackend=\"matplotlib\",\u003cbr\u003e\ncmap=\"jet\",\u003cbr\u003e\nprojection=ccrs.PlateCarree(),\u003cbr\u003e\nfeatures=[\"borders\", \"coastline\"],\u003cbr\u003e\ntitle=f\"Hydrophobic BC - India Zoom\",\u003cbr\u003e\nxlim=(68, 98),  # Longitude bounds\u003cbr\u003e\nylim=(6, 36),   # Latitude bounds\u003cbr\u003e\n)\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eResulting in the following MPAS-GOCART2G Instructions","","The instructions are tailored for running MPAS-GOCART2G on the NSF NCAR HPC system called Derecho.","","These instructions combine information from both the \u003ca href=\"https://www2.mmm.ucar.edu/projects/mpas/tutorial/Virtual2026/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noopener noreferrer\"\u003eMPAS Tutorial - Practice Guide\u003c/a\u003e and the specific instructions for running with GOCART-2G aerosols. Be sure to go to the \u003ca href=\"https://mpas-dev.github.io/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noopener noreferrer\"\u003eMPAS Home page\u003c/a\u003e and \u003ca href=\"https://www2.mmm.ucar.edu/projects/mpas/site/index.html\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noopener noreferrer\"\u003eMPAS-Atmosphere webpage\u003c/a\u003e where there is a lot more information and documentation.","","## **Table of Contents**","","**Background chapter** provides a brief description of the MPAS-GOCART-2G model. ","","**Chapter 1** gives instructions on running a case where all the input files are provided. It allows the new user to become familiar with the workflow in preparing a MPAS-GOCART2G simulation and is great for ensuring that the user has configured their system properly. ","","**Chapter 2** provides information on setting up your own case using the same grid mesh as in chapter 1 but running a different time period, including where to obtain input files and running the program *init\\_atmosphere* which sets up the MPAS-GOCART2G simulation. ","","**Chapter 3** provides information on running with a variable resolution grid mesh and setting up different variable resolution grids. ","","**Chapter 4** provides instructions for viewing MPAS-GOCART2G output using uxarray in Python scripts. ","","**Appendix A** explains how to download MERRA2 files.","","**Appendix B** describes ways to visualize MPAS-GOCART2G output.","","### Quick workflow guide for first-time users","","If you are new to MPAS-GOCART2G, use this order:","","1. Read the background section only to understand the model and terminology.","2. Complete Chapter 1 to build and test the example case with pre-provided inputs.","3. Use Chapter 2 to prepare the files needed for your own case.","4. Once the case is set up, verify that the generated `namelist.*` and `streams.*` files are in the run directory.","5. Submit the job and check model output files before trying a larger simulation.","","## **Background chapter Familiarizing yourself with the physics suite to run a MPAS-GOCART2G simulation**","","The MPAS-GOCART2G model has been developed as part of the NSF NCAR’s vision to move towards a unified modeling framework and links the MPAS-A dynamical core (Skamarock et al., 2012) to the GOCART2G chemical mechanism (Collow et al., 2024). The GOCART2G module adds 30 new scalars to represent eight tropospheric aerosol species and their precursors. Major aerosol species represented by GOCART2G include black carbon (BC), brown carbon (BrC), organic carbon (OC), sulfate, nitrate, ammonium, dust, and sea-salt. BC, BrC, and OC are present in hydrophobic and hydrophilic modes with an e-folding lifetime of 2.5 days for conversion of hydrophobic mode to hydrophilic mode. All the aerosol species are subjected to gravitational settling, dry deposition, and wet deposition processes. Gravitational settling is calculated following the parameterization of Fuchs (1965). Dry deposition velocity of aerosol and precursor species is estimated using the Wesely (1989) scheme. Resolved scale wet deposition of aerosols is calculated using aerosol-aware Thompson-Eidhammer 2014 scheme. Convective transport of all chemical constituents and wet deposition is simulated using nTiedtke scheme.  ","","The implementation of GOCART-2G is illustrated in Figure 1 and can be categorized into four layers as the Input Layer, the Model Initialization and Temporal Update Layer, The Physics and Dynamics Layer, and the Chemistry and Diagnostics layer:","","```{figure} static/MPAS_GOCART2G_Inst_F1.png",":alt: The MPAS-A, chemistry, and external tools system flowchart",":align: center",":width: 100%","","**Figure 1.** The MPAS-A system (gray and blue boxes) and chemistry and GOCART-2G system (green and yellow boxes).","```","","Anthropogenic emissions are represented using the CAMS emissions inventory (Soulie et al., 2023). BC emissions from all sources are assumed to be 80% in hydrophobic mode while BrC and OC emissions are assumed to be 50% in hydrophobic mode at the time of emissions. The remaining fractions are considered to be hydrophilic. Anthropogenic emissions from the aviation sector (if provided as input to the model) are distributed in three layers: Landing and Take off emissions are assigned to the 100 m layer, Continuous Climb and Descent Operations (CDO) emissions are assigned to 100 m \\- 9 km layer and the remaining aircraft flight operations are distributed between 9 and 10 km altitude layers. Emissions from all other anthropogenic sources are emitted within the lowest 100 m. ","","Biogenic emissions are read from pre-calculated emissions files available in the CAMS inventory (i.e., they are *not* computed at each emission time step with inputs from local temperature and PAR). CAMS v4 biogenic emissions include only isoprene emissions, therefore CAMS v3.1 is used and CAMS 2019 biogenic emissions are applied to MPAS-GOCART2G simulations for case studies for all years\\. Following Collow et al. (2024), isoprene, monoterpene, and other terpene emissions are assigned to the hydrophilic mode of the OC. Since their emissions occur at the tree top, they are only emitted in the lowest model layer.   ","","Biomass burning emissions in MPAS-GOCART-2G are represented using FINN version 2.5.1 (Wiedinmyer et al., 2023). A climatological diurnal profile is applied to distribute the emissions to hourly values. MPAS-GOCART-2G then uniformly mixes the biomass burning emissions within the PBL. To ensure model stability and prevent unrealistically high Aerosol Optical Depth (AOD) values from biomass burning, the model limits biomass burning emissions in such a way that AOD from biomass burning emissions over all time steps in a day cannot exceed AOD = 30.0.  ","","Dust aerosols are represented using five size bins (radii of 0.73, 1.4, 2.4, 4.5, and 8.0 µm) and their emissions are calculated online within the model utilizing the Ginoux et al. (2001) parameterization but with the dimensionless factor C set to 0.65 µg s\u003csup\u003e2\u003c/sup\u003e m\u003csup\u003e-5\u003c/sup\u003e as in (Zhao et al., 2010). Static geographical fields such as “erod” (erodibility representing the areas from where dust aerosols can be emitted), clayfrac and sandfrac required for dust emissions are mapped to the MPAS-GOCART domain along with the processing of other static geographical fields (see Section 1.3 of \u003ca href=\"https://www2.mmm.ucar.edu/projects/mpas/tutorial/Virtual2026/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noopener noreferrer\"\u003eMPAS Tutorial - Practice Guide\u003c/a\u003e for processing these fields). Sea-salt aerosols also use five size bins, specifically with radii of 0.079, 0.316, 1.119, 2.818, and 7.772 µm. Emissions for sea-salt are calculated using the Gong (2003) wind-driven parameterization, but this includes two key modifications: first, friction velocity has replaced the 10m wind speed, which is required for tuning the parameterization's constants; and second, a correction term dependent on sea surface temperature was added. This temperature-dependent modification is similar to the approach by Jaegle et al. (2011) but was specifically tuned to improve agreement between the simulated sea-salt AOD and MODIS-retrieved AOD. The GOCART2G’s default scale-aware factor of 0.6 at grid spacings exceeding 200 km to 0.35 at grid spacings less than 12 km has been applied to model sea-salt emissions as a function of model grid spacing.
